# Concept map — what the book states, where it is coded, where it is taught, how we know it is right

One row per implemented concept, theorem or numbered equation. Never delete a verified row; if a later chapter
supersedes an implementation, edit the row and add "superseded chNN". Test names are families in `tests/test_chNN.py`
(e.g. `test_hydrostatics_*`). "nb §1.7 · C20" = the notebook section and the 🧩 CORE block that teaches it.
Explainer short names: ch01 E1 `continuum_averaging_volume`, E2 `viscosity_momentum_diffusion`, E3 `heat_work_paths`,
E4 `parcel_stability`, E5 `buckingham_pi_machine`; ch02 E1 `rotation_of_axes`, E2 `cauchy_traction_principal_axes`,
E3 `strain_vs_rotation_split`, E4 `gauss_flux_box`, E5 `stokes_circulation_loop`; ch03 E1 `flow_lines_unsteady`, E2
`material_derivative_probe`, E3 `galilean_frames_cylinder`, E4 `fluid_element_deformation`, E5
`spin_and_principal_axes`, E6 `vortex_paddle_wheels`, E7 `reynolds_transport_cv`; ch04 E1 `control_volume_budgets`, E2
`stream_function_spacing`, E3 `newtonian_stress_lab`, E4 `navier_stokes_term_balance`, E5 `rotating_frame_coriolis`, E6
`which_bernoulli`, E7 `viscous_dissipation_heating`, E8 `boussinesq_buoyancy`, E9 `dynamic_similarity_models`; ch05 E1
`vortex_tubes_cannot_end`, E2 `vortex_pressure_funnel`, E3 `kelvin_material_loop`, E4 `baroclinic_torque`, E5
`vorticity_stretching_tilting`, E6 `biot_savart_filament`, E7 `vorticity_equation_rotating`, E8 `point_vortex_lab`, E9
`vortex_sheet_rollup`; ch06 E1 `superposition_sandbox`, E2 `cylinder_circulation_lift`, E3 `vortex_wall_images`, E4 `complex_potential_corners`, E5 `blasius_kutta_contour`, E6 `conformal_joukowski`, E7 `laplace_relaxation`, E8 `axial_singularity_bodies`, E9 `added_mass_sphere`; ch07 E1 `dispersion_relation`, E2 `particle_orbits`, E3 `capillary_gravity_waves`, E4 `seiche_standing_waves`, E5 `group_velocity_packets`, E6 `wave_rays_refraction`, E7 `hydraulic_jump`, E8 `two_layer_modes`, E9 `internal_wave_beams`; ch08 E1 `couette_poiseuille_backflow`, E2 `lubrication_scaling`, E3 `slider_bearing`, E4 `viscous_gravity_current`, E5 `stokes_first_problem`, E6 `similarity_exponents`, E7 `oscillating_plate`, E8 `stokes_sphere_flow`, E9 `stokes_drag_settling`; ch09 E1 `bl_scaling_thicknesses`, E2 `blasius_similarity_collapse`, E3 `falkner_skan_family`, E4 `thwaites_marching`, E5 `cylinder_drag_crisis`, E6 `karman_street_stability`, E7 `free_jet_similarity`, E8 `wall_jet_invariant`, E9 `teacup_secondary_flow`; ch10 E1 `fd_stencil_order`, E2 `von_neumann_amplification`, E3 `upwind_cfl_advection`, E4 `cell_peclet_wiggles`, E5 `fem_hat_assembly`, E6 `mac_projection_staggered`, E7 `lid_driven_cavity`, E8 `mixed_fe_lbb`. CORE, NOTE and D ids restart in every chapter, so ch02 rows say "ch02 C05" / "ch02 D05". ch09 module aliases: BL = `core.boundary_layer`, BB = `core.bluff_body`, JET = `core.jets`, SR = `core.similarity_reduce` (all re-exported by `ch09`). ch10 module aliases: FD = `core.fd`, FEM1 = `core.fem1d`, MAC = `core.mac`, MCK = `core.maccormack`, FEM2 = `core.fem2d` (all re-exported by `ch10`). ch10 printed slips are "R1…R12" in code and analysis but "slip #1…#12" in the notebook (recap IDs are R01…R13). ch11 E1 `normal_mode_growth`, E2 `kelvin_helmholtz_boundary`, E3 `benard_neutral_curve`, E4 `salt_fingers`, E5 `taylor_couette_onset`, E6 `richardson_shear_instability`, E7 `inviscid_shear_criteria`, E8 `orr_sommerfeld_neutral_curve`, E9 `lorenz_attractor`. ch11 module alias: ST = `core.stability` (re-exported by `ch11`). ch11 printed slips are S1…S13 in code and analysis, "slip #1…#13" in the notebook (SKIP ids are S01, S02). ch12 E1 `reynolds_averaging_window`, E2 `correlation_and_spectrum`, E3 `reynolds_stress_parcels`, E4 `energy_cascade_spectrum`, E5 `turbulent_energy_budget`, E6 `turbulent_jet_similarity`, E7 `law_of_the_wall`, E8 `mixing_length_closure`, E9 `stratified_surface_layer`, E10 `taylor_dispersion`. ch12 module aliases: TS = `core.turbstats`, WT = `core.wall_turbulence` (both re-exported by `ch12`). ch12 printed slips are "slip #1…#18" everywhere (`ch12.book_slips()`); A1–A5 = animations, F1–F8 = plotly slider figures.

`tests/ch11_verify_figures.py` (10 local verification figures + 2 from loop 2).

Named-only ch12 items without code of their own (words only, curation C tier or conceptual B): N03 the largest eddy is
about the layer thickness and the turbulent / irrotational interface is ragged (sketch from the synthetic field; → C07, C09,
C10), N04 scope — incompressible, no Coriolis force, three-dimensional fluctuations; nearly two-dimensional geostrophic
turbulence is deferred (**→ Ch. 13: 2-D turbulence sends energy upscale, the opposite of C07's cascade**), N05 a one-line
timeline that links each name to its block (Reynolds, Taylor, Prandtl, von Kármán, Richardson, Kolmogorov, Obukhov), N41
why the mean is what matters, N55 passive scalar (with `mixture_density`), N61 with Reynolds stresses the mean momentum
equation has no Bernoulli integral (→ ch04 `which_bernoulli`), N98 the first confirmation of the −5/3 law in a tidal
channel (the number private), N99 universality of the small scales motivates closures and large-eddy simulation, N127 and
N129 profiles and energy-budget terms across a plane jet (qualitative model panels), N155 open questions on the overlap
layer — the sublayer is universal, the wake is not (taught through the fit window in C11), N179 the closed set, wall
functions and their limits, Reynolds-stress closures, N217 concluding remarks with pointers to Ch. 13 (rotating,
stratified, geostrophic turbulence); S01 exercises, S02 literature. Two "one quarter" statements are kept apart in words:
Ri > ¼ everywhere ⇒ linear stability (ch11 (11.67), a theorem) and Rf_cr ≈ ¼ (an observation; by
$\mathrm{Ri}=(\nu_T/\kappa_T)\mathrm{Rf}$ (12.109) it corresponds to Ri = Pr_T/4).

### Reusable derivation moves (point to the D row instead of re-deriving the move)
| Move | First shown in |
|---|---|
| Divide Newton II by the body's own mass/density, then Taylor-expand the surroundings to first order | ch01 D18 |
| Eliminate a path function between two relations to leave state-function relations | ch01 D10 |
| Set ds = 0 in both Gibbs forms and divide them so the unknown cancels | ch01 D14 |
| Introduce g = h − Ts to get a Maxwell relation | ch01 D19 |
| Log-differentiate a product of powers into a sum of relative rates | ch01 D36 |
| Try e^{λt} to turn a linear ODE into algebra; treat the double root separately | ch01 D18 |
| Integrate pressure over a box's faces: sides cancel, top minus bottom is the net force | ch01 D05, D37 |
| "Dimensionless ⇔ exponent vector in the null space", then rank–nullity | ch01 D28 |
| Take the dot product with a basis vector and let orthonormality (δ) kill all terms but one | ch02 D01, D03 |
| Completeness Σ e'_j e'_jᵀ = I turns "a vector is the sum of its projections" into CCᵀ = I | ch02 D02 |
| Write the same law in two frames; "true for every n ⇒ the coefficient matrix vanishes" | ch02 D06 |
| A contracted pair of C's is δ, so any closed index chain (trace, A_ij A_ji, det) is frame-independent | ch02 D18 |
| Shrink an element: face terms ∝ h², volume terms ∝ h³; divide by the area (or volume) and let h → 0 | ch02 D05, D21 |
| Contract two ε's with (2.19), remembering δ_ii = 3 (so 3δ − δ = 2δ); a one-place index move flips the sign | ch02 D09, D15 |
| Enumerate ε for one component; the other two follow by cyclic relabelling (say so, never "similarly") | ch02 D12 |
| Add and subtract ½Bᵀ; uniqueness by "X = −X ⇒ X = 0" | ch02 D14 |
| Multiply the eigen-equation by the conjugate eigenvector to show λ is real; say exactly where symmetry is used | ch02 D17 |
| Expand n in the eigenbasis: n·τ·n = Σ λ^k c_k², a weighted mean, hence the bounds | ch02 D17 |
| FTC along one axis turns a volume integral of a derivative into two face integrals | ch02 D25 |
| Tile a region: shared faces (edges) appear twice with opposite normals (directions) and cancel | ch02 D25, D26 |
| First-order Taylor about a face centre; the midpoint value is the face average for linear variation | ch02 D22 |
| Walk a small rectangle side by side; opposite sides differ by one derivative times the side length | ch02 D26 |
| Invert the Lagrangian map for the label, then substitute to get the Eulerian field | ch03 D01 |
| Chain rule along a particle path with dr/dt = u; "every point hosts a particle" turns a path identity into a field identity | ch03 D02 |
| Parallel ⇔ cross product zero ⇒ the ratio form (and why the cross-product form is safer) | ch03 D03 |
| Integrate, fix the constants by the passing condition, eliminate the parameter with sin² + cos² = 1 | ch03 D04, D05 |
| Label a streak line by its release time t_o and eliminate t_o, not t | ch03 D05 |
| Moving-frame chain rule: ∂/∂t at fixed x = ∂/∂t′ at fixed x′ − U·∇′ | ch03 D06 |
| Taylor-expand the neighbour's velocity to first order; name the O(\|dx\|²) remainder | ch03 D07 |
| Track a material element's ends for dt, divide by its length (or angle), let dt → 0 | ch03 D08, D09, D12 |
| Small angle tan ε ≈ ε; stretching changes the angles only at second order | ch03 D09, D12 |
| Define spin as the average rate of two perpendicular lines, then show it is the same for every pair | ch03 D12, D14 |
| Subtract the frame's rigid velocity Ω × x and use ∇×(Ω × x) = 2Ω | ch03 D10, D13 |
| Three-factor product rule plus det(I + G dt) = 1 + tr G dt | ch03 D11 |
| One ε index swap flips a cross product: ε_ijk ω_k dx_j = −(ω × dx)_i | ch03 D15 |
| Circulation round a small polar sector ÷ area; the arcs r dθ and (r + dr) dθ differ, giving ∂(ru_θ)/∂r | ch03 D17 |
| Stokes on a circle plus the substitution s = r²/σ² | ch03 D19 |
| Extremum: derivative zero, clear denominators, drop the trivial root, bracket and solve (brentq / Lambert W) | ch03 D20 |
| Antiderivative Φ with ∂Φ/∂x = F, then the chain rule on Φ(b(t), t) − Φ(a(t), t) | ch03 D21 |
| New volume = old volume + signed swept sliver; Taylor in time; the Δt·ΔV term is O(Δt²) and drops | ch03 D22 |
| Set F = 1 and b = u, apply Gauss, shrink the volume (mean-value theorem) | ch03 D23 |
| Product rule ∇·(Fu) = u·∇F + F∇·u exposes the hidden F∇·u term | ch03 D24 |
| **Coincident control volume**: write the law for a material volume, pick V* equal to it at one instant; same region + same integrand + same instant ⇒ equal ∂/∂t integrals and fluxes, while the d/dt∫ terms differ; subtract the RTT for V* | ch04 D01, D05 (and (4.48) a-D29) |
| Gauss turns every surface integral into a volume integral, then **localisation** (true for every volume + continuous integrand ⇒ pointwise) | ch04 D02, D07, D19 |
| Flux (conservative) form → advective form: expand ∂(ρF)/∂t + ∇·(ρuF) and subtract F × continuity (never F × ∇·u alone) | ch04 D07 (F = u_j), D20 (F = E) |
| Keep the stress's **first** index on the normal (f_j = n_iτ_ij) through Gauss; test with a non-symmetric τ | ch04 D07, D19 |
| Torque on a shrinking element: torque ∝ h³, moment of inertia ∝ h⁵ ⇒ angular acceleration ∝ 1/h² unless the coefficient vanishes | ch04 D08 |
| Isotropy collapses a tensor law: write the most general isotropic K from δ products, contract with a symmetric S, keep only the sums that act | ch04 D09 |
| Add and subtract ⅔μS_mmδ_ij to split shape change (deviator) from volume change (trace) | ch04 D10, D23 |
| Swap mixed partials (Schwarz) to turn ∂_i∂_j u_i into ∂_j(∇·u), then let ∇·u = 0 kill it | ch04 D12 |
| Curl of a curl from ε–δ: ∇×∇×u = ∇(∇·u) − ∇²u, so μ∇²u = −μ∇×ω when ∇·u = 0 | ch04 D13 |
| Differentiate a vector written on a turning basis: de′/dt = Ω × e′, then product rule twice; the factor 2 is two separate Ω × u′ | ch04 D14, D15 |
| Move frame (acceleration) terms to the force side: every sign flips (+2Ω × u′ → −2Ω × u′) | ch04 D16 |
| Small-time series of an exact rotating-frame path: ut sin Ωt ≈ Ωut² | ch04 D17 |
| Recognise a conservative apparent force: −Ω × (Ω × x) = Ω²R e_R = −∇(−½Ω²R²), fold it into an effective potential | ch04 D18 (then D24 for gravity) |
| Energy equation from momentum: dot Cauchy with u (chain rule D(½u²)/Dt = u·Du/Dt); subtract from total energy to leave internal energy | ch04 D21, D22 |
| Prove a sign by completing the square: rewrite σ:S as a sum of squares with non-negative coefficients (needs δ_ijδ_ij = 3) | ch04 D23 |
| Lamb identity (u·∇)u = −u × ω + ∇(½u²): an advective term becomes a gradient plus a term ⟂ u and ω; then dot with u or ω | ch04 D24, D25 |
| A bracket whose gradient vanishes depends on t only; absorb it by a gauge change of φ (check the sign by substituting back) | ch04 D26 |
| Subtract the hydrostatic base state, then scale: keep ρ′ only where it multiplies g (g ≫ \|Du/Dt\|) | ch04 D27 |
| Pressure work of a perfect gas p∇·u = ρR DT/Dt folds C_v into C_p | ch04 D28 |
| A surface point keeps η = 0 ⇒ chain rule along its path ⇒ Dη/Dt = 0 (only the normal part of the surface velocity is defined) | ch04 D29 |
| Scale every variable by the problem's sizes, bring out each derivative's factor (∂/∂t = Ω∂/∂t*, ∇ = ∇*/l), divide by the advective scale ρU²/l: the brackets are the groups | ch04 D30 |
| Build an element balance with first-order changes, expand with sympy, drop (ds)² terms, divide by ds | ch04 D06 |
| Gauss on a piece of tube whose side is made of the field's own lines (ω·n = 0 on the side): the two lids carry equal and opposite flux | ch05 D01 |
| Integrate a partial derivative: the "constant" is a function of the other variable, fixed by the other equation (then by a reference point) | ch05 D02 |
| Polar metric factor before declaring a force zero: (1/r²)∂(r²σ_rθ)/∂r, never ∂σ_rθ/∂r | ch05 D03 |
| Parametrise a material loop by fixed particle labels so D/Dt passes inside ∮; then D(dx)/Dt = du | ch05 D04 |
| ∮dF = 0 needs a single-valued F: for ∮dp/ρ that means ρ = ρ(p); otherwise Stokes turns it into −∫(∇ρ × ∇p/ρ²)·n dA | ch05 D05, D19 |
| Torque about the centre of mass (move the reference point: M_G = M_O + (r_O − r_G) × F), ÷ moment of inertia, × 2 = vorticity rate | ch05 D06 |
| Evaluate a smoothed-step source at its centre to get an exact local rate (mean density there) | ch05 D07 |
| Kelvin on every surface lying on a tube wall ⇒ the wall stays a wall (localisation "for every S") | ch05 D08 |
| Take the curl of a momentum equation: every gradient term (pressure with ρ const, gravity, ½u²) dies | ch05 D09, D15 |
| Green's function of ∇² from the flux of ∇(1/r) through a small sphere (−4π), then superposition | ch05 D10 |
| Differentiate 1/\|x − x′\| with respect to the *source* point: ∇′ = −∇ (the book's sign slip lives here) | ch05 D10, D11 |
| Product rule for a curl + Gauss in curl form to trade a volume term for a surface term that a well-chosen volume kills | ch05 D11 |
| Freeze a slowly varying kernel across a thin core (relative error ≈ 2a/distance) and integrate the core alone | ch05 D12 |
| Substitution l = −d cot θ turns a segment integral into ∫sin θ dθ; let the ends run to infinity as a limit | ch05 D13 |
| Index-notation curl with ε–δ, **keeping the term the book drops** until a stated constraint (∇·u = 0) kills it | ch05 D15 |
| Project (ω·∇)u onto the natural frame of a vortex line: along = stretching, across = tilting | ch05 D16, D17 |
| Rotating frame in a loop integral: (Ω × u)·dx = Ω·(u × dx) and ∮(Ω × x)·dx = 2Ω·A_vec | ch05 D19 |
| Γ_a of a thin column + mass Ah = const ⇒ (ζ + f)/h conserved | ch05 D20 |
| Lever rule for the centre of vorticity; a conserved ΣΓx keeps it fixed | ch05 D21 |
| Mirror image of opposite sign enforces no penetration on a plane wall; the image's velocity at the vortex is the drift | ch05 D22 |
| Circulation of a thin box across a jump: long sides give (u₂ − u₁)ds, short sides vanish as dn → 0 | ch05 D23 |
| Flux of ∇ ln r through every circle is 2π while ∇² ln r = 0 away from 0 ⇒ ∇² ln r = 2πδ (the delta is the flux, not the pointwise value) | ch06 D03 |
| Rotate a gradient by 90°: ∇ψ = e_z × ∇φ gives orthogonality and \|∇φ\| = \|∇ψ\| in one line | ch06 D04 |
| Limit with a product held fixed (2mε = \|d\|): Taylor the log to first order, let ε → 0 and m → ∞ together; the ε² terms cancel | ch06 D06 |
| Choose the branch so the cut lies inside the body (θ-branch of the half-body, `log_branch(cut=)`) before reading ψ on the dividing streamline | ch06 D07 |
| Mass balance far downstream (width × U = m) as an independent check of a θ → 0 limit | ch06 D07 |
| Expand the squared surface speed fully; period integrals (∫sin, ∫sin³, ∫sin cos = 0, ∫sin² = π) keep only the cross term | ch06 D11 |
| Vieta r₊r₋ = a² tells which quadratic root is physical (one inside, one outside the body) | ch06 D10, D21 |
| Parity in y: an odd ψ (vortex image of opposite sign) or an even φ (source image of the same sign) makes the wall a streamline | ch06 D12 |
| ∂/∂t at a fixed point of a field that moves with a body = −u_s·∇ (∂/∂x_s = −∇) + explicit time dependence | ch06 D13, D29 |
| Difference quotient along x vs along iy: equal limits ⇔ Cauchy–Riemann; then the velocity is the conjugate of dw/dz | ch06 D14 |
| Tangency on the body: (u + iv)dz* = (u − iv)dz (parallel **or** antiparallel — the ± appears on both sides and cancels) | ch06 D17 |
| Write the force as a contour integral of an analytic function, then trade the body for any enclosing contour (Cauchy); detect contours that cut the body | ch06 D17 |
| Laurent series of the far field + residue theorem: only the 1/z coefficient survives ∮ (the rest, including a slip in 1/z², drops) | ch06 D18 |
| Chain rule through a map: dw/dz = (dw/dζ)(dζ/dz), with dζ/dz = 1/(dz/dζ); take the root outside the circle (√(z − 2b)√(z + 2b), cut on the slit) | ch06 D21 |
| Taylor at half-points: the average rule and its truncation (Δx²/12)ψ_xxxx; a re-entrant corner (n < 1 in Azⁿ) lowers the observed order to 2n | ch06 D22, D15 |
| Spectral radius of the sweep matrix ⇒ sweeps k ≈ ln(tol/r₀)/ln ρ; stop on a residual far below the wanted accuracy | ch06 D23 |
| Substitution z − ξ = R cot α at a fixed field point: dξ = R dα/sin²α, the two minus signs cancel and the limits keep their order | ch06 D26 |
| Collocation: N conditions at N points → N × N system; check the condition number and a moment (Σk_nΔξ, dipole), not the individual strengths | ch06 D27 |
| Integrate a surface pressure on a sphere: azimuth first kills cos φ, sin φ; ∫cos²θ sin θ dθ = 2/3 | ch06 D30 |
| Green's first identity: ∇²φ = 0 makes ½ρ\|∇φ\|² a divergence; the fluid's outward normal points into the body; a far sphere contributes ~R⁻³ → 0 | ch06 D31 |
| Taylor-transfer a condition from the moving boundary z = η to the flat z = 0 and size the dropped terms from the forcing (surface rises at aω ⇒ velocities aω, φ ~ aω/k) — relative error ka | ch07 D03, D04 |
| Product trial + the boundary conditions pick sin vs cos in the phase (each condition differentiates η once) | ch07 D05 |
| Regroup Ae^{kz} + Be^{−kz} with B = Ae^{−2kH} into 2Ae^{−kH}cosh k(z + H): the bottom condition is then automatic | ch07 D05 |
| Match coefficients of cos(kx − ωt) because the identity holds for all x, t (the dispersion relation falls out) | ch07 D06, D28 |
| Minimise c² instead of c (same point, no square roots); at the minimum the tangent passes through the origin, so c_g = c | ch07 D16 |
| Sum-to-product splits two waves into a fast carrier times a slow envelope (beats) or a fixed-node pattern (standing wave) | ch07 D17, D19 |
| Taylor-expand ω(k) about k₀ inside a Fourier integral: the linear term shifts the envelope at c_g, the quadratic term spreads it (valid for t ≪ 1/(ω″δk²)) | ch07 D20 |
| Mixed partials of a phase θ(x, t) give a conservation law for k (∂k/∂t + ∂ω/∂x = 0); read it along characteristics | ch07 D22 |
| In a steady medium ω changes in time only through k: multiply crest conservation by c_g (not c) ⇒ ω constant along rays; add dk/dt = −∂ω/∂x (Hamilton) to bend them | ch07 D23, D24 |
| A property independent of y conserves k_y ⇒ \|k\| sin α = const (Snell) | ch07 D24 |
| CV momentum across a jump, cancel the non-zero factor (H₁ − H₂), divide by gH₁³ ⇒ quadratic in H₂/H₁; Vieta picks the positive root | ch07 D25 |
| Taylor the Eulerian velocity about the particle's mean position and average over a period: the O(a²) mean is the Stokes drift (sin² + cos² makes it time-independent) | ch07 D27 |
| Complex amplitudes turn multi-layer BCs into a linear system; factor the solvability condition (sympy `factor`) and keep the "trivial" factor — it is the surface mode | ch07 D29, D30 |
| Apply ∂/∂t, ∇_H², ∂/∂z to whole linear equations and substitute until one unknown is left (derivatives commute, also with N(z)) | ch07 D33 |
| Plane-wave substitution ∂/∂x → ik, ∂/∂t → −iω, ∇² → −K²; when ω depends on the direction of K only, ∇_K ω ⟂ K (c ⟂ c_g) | ch07 D34, D36 |
| Mean of a product of real parts ⟨Re(Ae^{iθ})Re(Be^{iθ})⟩ = ½Re(AB*): 90° out of phase (B = iA) carries no mean product | ch07 D37 |
| Continuity + a wall value: ∂v/∂y = 0 only says v is constant across the gap; the wall fixes it to 0, so the advective term vanishes **exactly** and the problem is linear | ch08 D02 |
| "A function of x alone equals a function of y alone ⇒ both are the same constant" (differentiate each side by the other variable) | ch08 D03 |
| Integrate twice and fix two constants from two conditions; regularity at the axis (drop A ln R) counts as one condition | ch08 D04, D06, D08 |
| Where a flow reverses, the wall slope changes sign: set du/dy(0) = 0 for the threshold, du/dy = 0 for the extremum | ch08 D05 |
| A force balance on a control volume gives a wall stress without the profile (πa²Δp = −2πaLτ₀), so it survives in turbulent means | ch08 D07 |
| Trial R^λ turns an Euler–Cauchy ODE into a polynomial in λ (roots ±1 for circular Couette; {4, 2, 1, −1} for Stokes' f) | ch08 D08, D29 |
| Scale with two lengths (L along, h = εL across), take the cross velocity scale from continuity (v ~ εU), multiply each equation so one chosen term has coefficient 1 and read the others' powers of ε | ch08 D10 |
| Take a limit on the scaled equation, then re-dimensionalise — the step that restores dropped factors (the ν the page omits) | ch08 D11, D26 |
| Integrate continuity across a moving gap: the Leibniz boundary term −u(h)∂h/∂x cancels against the kinematic wall condition, leaving ∂h/∂t + ∂q/∂x = 0 | ch08 D13 |
| Go to the frame where the flow is steady (the pad frame): the flux is then one constant, and two end pressures fix it | ch08 D14 |
| Dimensional analysis plus linearity leave one similarity variable; three conditions collapsing into two (t → 0 and y → ∞ both give η → ∞) is the test that the form is right | ch08 D17, D18 |
| Substitute a power-law ansatz At^{−n}F(ξ/Dt^m), demand every bracket carry the same power of t, and fix the second exponent from a conserved integral (wall speed, jump, circulation, volume) | ch08 D21, D22, D23 |
| Spot an exact derivative F + ηF′ = (ηF)′ and integrate once; the constant vanishes because F and ηF → 0 far away | ch08 D22 |
| Complex trial Re{e^{iωt}f(y)} for a periodic boundary; √i = (1 + i)/√2; keep only the bounded root (the discarded one grows like e^{+y/δ}) | ch08 D24 |
| Choose a quantity's scale by dominant balance: if pressure balances viscous stress, p − p∞ ~ μU/L, not ρU² (the wrong scale makes a term vanish in the limit) | ch08 D26 |
| Take the curl to eliminate the pressure; afterwards recover p by a line integral of ∇p and **check the other component** | ch08 D27, D30 |
| Project the surface traction on the stream axis (t_x = σ_rr cos θ − σ_rθ sin θ) and integrate with dA = 2πa² sin θ dθ; split pressure and friction parts | ch08 D31 |
| Far from a body compare **magnitudes** of the vector terms, keeping each term's slowest-decaying part (Ua/r, not a³/r³), to find where an approximation fails and with what prefactor | ch08 D32 |
| Expand 1 − e^{−s} = s − s²/2 + … where s is small to show an improved solution reduces to the simpler one near the body | ch08 D33 |
| Stretch the thin coordinate (and the cross velocity) by Re^{1/2} so viscosity and advection balance; divide each equation by its advective scale and read the Re powers; drop only terms whose coefficient → 0 multiplying O(1) quantities | ch09 D01 |
| Bernoulli just outside the layer + ∂p/∂y = 0 across it hands the outer pressure gradient to every height (−(1/ρ)dp/dx = U_eU_e′) | ch09 D02 |
| Define a thickness by comparing the real flux with the flux of an ideal (slip) flow through the same section — mass gives δ*, momentum gives θ | ch09 D03, D04 |
| A control volume whose roof is a streamline carries no mass or momentum flux through the roof; the wall force equals the momentum lost across the outlet | ch09 D04 |
| Chain rule for a similarity variable that moves with x: η = y/δ(x) ⇒ ∂η/∂x = −ηδ′/δ, ∂η/∂y = 1/δ; a product δ(x)f(η) also needs the product rule | ch09 D05, D07, D16, D20 |
| After substitution, two brackets that depend on x only must be proportional (constant ratio) — that ODE for δ(x) fixes the growth law (C = 2 only names δ) | ch09 D05 |
| Use an ODE's scaling symmetry f → λf(λη) to turn a boundary-value problem into one initial-value problem and a rescaling (Töpfer) | ch09 D06, D21 |
| Integrate the ODE from the wall to η, then integrate by parts, to trade a wall derivative (f″(0)) for a thickness integral; let η → ∞ only after the boundary terms are shown to decay | ch09 D06 |
| Integrate across the layer on [0, h] and let h → ∞ only at the end when the individual pieces (∫u², ∫U_eU_e′) diverge but their combination converges | ch09 D08 |
| Multiply a first-order linear ODE by the integrating factor that makes its left side one derivative (U_e⁶ for Thwaites, f for the wall jet) | ch09 D09, D21 |
| Evaluate the momentum equation at the wall, where no-slip kills advection: the wall curvature equals the pressure gradient (sign of dp/dx = sign of u_yy) | ch09 D12 |
| Drag of a body = its surface pressure weighted by the stream-wise component of the normal (cos φ), integrated round it; the ideal (fore–aft symmetric) part cancels | ch09 D13 |
| Sum a periodic row of singularities in ±n pairs (the sum converges only paired) into a closed form (cotangent, 1/sin²) | ch09 D14 |
| Linear stability of a steady arrangement: perturb, keep terms linear in the displacement, write ẋ = Mx, factorise det(M − μI); growth = the largest real part | ch09 D14 |
| Before integrating an ODE, look for the total derivative ((ff′)′ = f′² + ff″, (3f′ + f²/2)′ = 3f″ + ff′) and integrate for free; fix the constants from the far field | ch09 D17, D21 |
| When the obvious flux is lost to a wall, integrate from y to ∞, multiply by u and integrate by parts with a variable lower limit: the next-order flux (Ψ) is the invariant | ch09 D19 |
| Integrate by partial fractions (`apart`) to an implicit η(g), then invert numerically with `brentq` | ch09 D21 |
| Thin layer: pressure set by the fast outer flow acts unchanged on the slowed fluid; compare it with the layer's own inertial force to find the secondary flow | ch09 D22 |
| Add and subtract the neighbours' Taylor series so the unwanted derivatives cancel; the first surviving term (divided by the step power) is the error and its exponent is the order | ch10 D01 |
| Substitute the exact solution into the discrete scheme and Taylor-expand in both t and x (write the time series the book skips); what is left is the truncation error | ch10 D03 |
| Trade time derivatives for space derivatives using the PDE itself (T_tt = u²T_xx …) to get the modified equation, then read off a numerical diffusivity or dispersion | ch10 D03, D17, D18 |
| The error obeys the same linear scheme; insert one Fourier mode e^{iθj}, divide by it and call the ratio G (amplification per step) | ch10 D04, D07, D08, D18 |
| Modulus of a complex expression as Re² + Im², then half-angle identities 1 − cos θ = 2 sin²(θ/2), sin θ = 2 sin(θ/2)cos(θ/2) | ch10 D05, D18 |
| Substitute s = sin²(θ/2) so a "for every θ" condition becomes linear in s ∈ [0, 1]; a linear function is ≤ 0 on an interval iff it is at both ends | ch10 D06 |
| For an implicit scheme G is 1/(bracket): bound the bracket's modulus from below by its real part (≥ 1 for β ≥ 0) | ch10 D07 |
| CFL as geometry: locate the foot of the characteristic x − uΔt relative to the stencil; stable iff it lies inside (interpolation, not extrapolation) | ch10 D08 |
| Multiply by a test function that vanishes where the data are prescribed, integrate, move one derivative by parts: the Neumann datum appears by itself (natural), the Dirichlet one must be built in (essential) | ch10 D09, D22 |
| "For every test function" ⇒ the integrand is zero (fundamental lemma); choose test functions vanishing at the boundary first, then the boundary term separately | ch10 D10 |
| Expand in a basis and demand the weak form for every basis function (unit coefficient vectors): one equation per unknown, a matrix system | ch10 D11 |
| Map every element to a parent interval/triangle, integrate once there (dx = (h/2)dξ, dΩ = J dξdη), then scatter-add into the global matrix | ch10 D13, D14 |
| Solve the discrete equation exactly: a geometric trial r^j turns a linear recurrence into a quadratic; a negative root means node-to-node alternation (wiggles) | ch10 D16 |
| Take the divergence of the correction step and impose ∇·u^{n+1} = 0: the pressure obeys a Poisson equation and the correction is a gradient (curl-free) | ch10 D19 |
| Sum the face fluxes of one cell; a wall face carries known (never corrected) velocity, so no pressure boundary condition appears; the row sums show the constant null vector (Σ rhs = 0, pin one value) | ch10 D20 |
| Plant a (−1)^{i+j} pattern into a stencil to find the modes it cannot see (count them with an SVD) | ch10 D21 |
| For a vector PDE dot with a test vector and use −∇p·ũ = −∇·(pũ) + p∇·ũ and D:∇ũ = D:D[ũ] (D symmetric) before the divergence theorem | ch10 D22 |
| Error model e = K h^p on three grids with ratio r: the ratio of successive differences gives r^p, then extrapolate f₀ ≈ f₁ + (f₁ − f₂)/(r^p − 1) | ch10 D23 |
| Write a mode two ways (e^{ikx + σt} and e^{ik(x − ct)}) and equate the exponents: σ = −ikc, so growth is kc_i and the frequency −kc_r | ch11 D01 |
| Transfer a condition from the disturbed interface z = ζ to z = 0 by a Taylor expansion and keep only O(ε); the O(1) balance is the base state and drops out | ch11 D02 |
| Insert decaying exponentials on each side of an interface, match the two conditions, and read stability from the **sign of the discriminant** of the resulting quadratic (real pair → conjugate pair) | ch11 D03, D04 |
| Eliminate the pressure from a Boussinesq momentum equation: take ∇² of the vertical component and subtract ∂_z of the divergence — only ∇_H² of the buoyancy survives | ch11 D06 |
| Multiply an eigen-equation by the complex conjugate of the unknown, integrate by parts with the boundary conditions, and take the imaginary part: σ_i (or c_i) times sign-definite integrals = 0 | ch11 D08, D18, D19, D22 |
| Apply an operator to one equation of a coupled pair and substitute the other: two second-order equations become one higher-order equation in a single unknown | ch11 D09, D21 |
| Trial e^{qz} → characteristic polynomial → general solution by parity → boundary conditions as a determinant = 0 → one neutral value per wavenumber → minimise over the wavenumber (critical point) | ch11 D10 |
| Differentiate with respect to K² (not K) and use the quotient rule to minimise a neutral curve given by a formula | ch11 D11 |
| "Same equations + same boundary conditions ⇒ proportional solutions": map a new problem onto a solved one by renaming the parameter (Rs − Ra ↔ Ra; Ta ↔ Ra) | ch11 D13, D15 |
| Narrow-gap (small-curvature) limit: write R = R₁ + xd, expand in d/R, keep the leading order; then rescale one unknown so the parameter appears once | ch11 D14, D15 |
| Substitute φ = ψ̂/(U − c)ⁿ with n chosen to make the equation self-adjoint (n = ½: Miles–Howard; n = 1: Howard), so integration by parts leaves no boundary term | ch11 D18, D19 |
| Turn two moment inequalities into a region of the complex plane by completing the square: (c_r − a)² + c_i² ≤ R² | ch11 D19 |
| Multiply the perturbation momentum equation by the perturbation velocity and average over a wavelength: divergence terms vanish (walls, periodicity) and production − dissipation remains | ch11 D23 |
| Galerkin truncation: insert a few modes, project with the orthogonality of sines, rescale amplitudes and time so the constants disappear (PDE → a few ODEs); then linearise about each fixed point — a cubic λ³ + a₂λ² + a₁λ + a₀ has roots ±iω exactly when a₂a₁ = a₀ (Hopf) | ch11 D24, D25 |
| Average a finite sum first and take N → ∞ last: every linear operation commutes with the sum, a product does not, so $\overline{\tilde u\tilde v}=\bar u\bar v+\overline{uv}$ | ch12 D01 |
| "A quadratic that is never negative has discriminant ≤ 0": apply it to $\overline{(u+\lambda v)^2}\ge0$ to bound a covariance | ch12 D02 |
| Shift the time (or space) origin under stationarity (homogeneity) to turn a two-time average into a function of the lag; evenness follows for the same variable, an index swap for two | ch12 D03 |
| Read a length or time scale off the osculating parabola of an even function at its maximum (second-order Taylor series) | ch12 D04 |
| Transform an even real function: the sine part vanishes, the transform is real and even, and its value at zero is the area of the function | ch12 D05 |
| Write the nonlinear term in flux form before averaging (add u_i × continuity), average with the linear rules, then return to advective form with mean continuity — the leftover covariance moves to the right as a stress | ch12 D06 |
| Fluctuation equation = total equation − mean equation; multiply by the fluctuation and average — everything multiplied by a mean alone averages away | ch12 D10 |
| Build the most general isotropic tensor from δ_ij and r_ir_j, read its two scalar functions by pointing r along one axis, then impose incompressibility ∂R_ij/∂r_j = 0 to tie them | ch12 D07 |
| Turn a mean product of gradients into derivatives of the two-point correlation at zero separation, $\overline{\partial_ku_i\,\partial_lu_j}=-\partial^2R_{ij}/\partial r_k\partial r_l\big\vert_0$ | ch12 D08 |
| Multiply the mean momentum equation by the mean velocity: U_i∂τ̄_ij/∂x_j = ∂(U_iτ̄_ij)/∂x_j − τ̄_ij∂U_i/∂x_j splits transport from the exchange and dissipation terms | ch12 D09 |
| Drop a variable from the list on physical grounds (no ν in the inertial range, no δ near the wall) and let the Π theorem leave a single group ⇒ a power law with fixed exponents | ch12 D11, D12, D18 |
| An integral invariant fixes one exponent and self-preservation (every bracket of the similarity equation constant) fixes the other | ch12 D13–D16 |
| Chain rule with a similarity variable that moves with x (ξ = y/δ(x)); V from continuity by integrating in ξ with ∫ξF′dξ = ξF − ∫F dξ | ch12 D14 |
| Match two descriptions in their overlap: multiply both gradients by y so that each side depends on a different variable ⇒ both equal a constant ⇒ a logarithm in each variable | ch12 D19 |
| Anchor a logarithmic profile at the height where it vanishes (roughness length) and convert it to a drag coefficient | ch12 D20 |
| Close a stress with a length: the momentum balance becomes a quadratic in the gradient — keep the positive root, then read the two limits (wall, far field) | ch12 D21 |
| Divide two rate equations to eliminate time and find one variable as a power of the other | ch12 D23 |
| Form a ratio of two budget terms and substitute closure relations to turn a flux ratio into a gradient ratio (Rf → Ri) or into a height (Rf = z/L_M) | ch12 D24, D25 |
| Write a displacement as the time integral of a velocity, differentiate the square, and pass the average inside the integral; a double integral over a triangle becomes a single integral with weight (t − τ) | ch12 D26 |
| Take both limits of a memory integral (r ≈ 1 for short times; upper limit → ∞ for long times) and keep the constant offset of the long-time line | ch12 D27, D28 |
